Use when the user runs /design-sync or says \"sync my design system to Claude Design\".","argumentHint":"[<project hint, e.g. \"Acme DS\">]","builtin":true},{"name":"dataviz","description":"Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. When the destination is a first-party document connector (host-designated, never self-described) that renders live charts, hand it the rows (inline, or as an uploaded data file the chart cites) rather than a rendered PNG/SVG — a picture of a chart loses hover, data inspection and per-value comments. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Triggers on: \"chart\", \"graph\", \"plot\", \"data viz\", \"visualization\", \"dashboard\", \"analytics\", \"visualize data\", \"categorical colors\", \"sequential / diverging palette\", \"stat tile\", \"sparkline\", \"heatmap\", \"legend\", \"axis\", \"tooltip\", \"chart colors\", \"color by series\".","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"update-config","description":"Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"verify","description":"Verify that a code change actually does what it's supposed to by exercising it end-to-end and observing behavior — drive the affected flow, not just tests or typecheck. Run before committing nontrivial changes; bootstraps this repo's project verify skill if none exists yet. Don't invoke it on a diff that only touches tests, docs, or other code with no runtime surface to drive (a change to product source always has one) — there's nothing to observe.","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"debug","description":"Enable debug logging for this session and help diagnose issues","argumentHint":"[issue description]","builtin":true},{"name":"code-review","description":"Review the current diff, or a PR number/branch/path target, for correctness bugs (plus reuse/simplification/efficiency cleanups where the model's review recipe covers them) at the given effort level (low/medium: fewer, high-confidence findings; high→max: broader coverage, may include uncertain findings; ultra: deep multi-agent review in the cloud); with no level given, it reuses the level you typed last. Pass --comment to post findings as inline PR comments, or --fix to apply the findings to the working tree after the review. For ultra on a GitHub.com PR target, --post asks to post the finished review’s findings to the PR as a single comment from the user’s GitHub account (not a review; the launch dialog still confirms in interactive sessions, while non-interactive mode posts on the flag alone) and --no-post hides that option.","argumentHint":"[low|medium|high|xhigh|max|ultra] [--fix] [--comment] [<pr#>|<branch>|<path>]","aliases":["review"],"builtin":true},{"name":"simplify","description":"Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.","argumentHint":"[<target>]","builtin":true},{"name":"batch","description":"Research and plan a large-scale change, then execute it in parallel across 5–30 isolated worktree agents that each open a PR.","argumentHint":"<instruction>","builtin":true},{"name":"fewer-permission-prompts","description":"Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"doctor","description":"Health-check the user's Claude Code setup and fix issues: diagnose installation health — what the `claude doctor` terminal diagnostics cover — from local data (duplicate or leftover installs, PATH, unparseable settings files, broken or colliding agent definitions, skills whose frontmatter fails to parse); find unused skills, MCP servers, and plugins versus their context cost and disable dead weight; deduplicate local CLAUDE.md files against checked-in ones; trim checked-in CLAUDE.md files by cutting content a session could derive from the codebase (directory layouts, tech-stack lists, architecture overviews) while keeping gotchas, rationale, and non-standard conventions; migrate always-loaded CLAUDE.md guidance into lazy skills and nested CLAUDE.md files; flag slow hooks and context-heavy extensions; check the installed version is current; make auto mode the default permission mode; and pre-approve frequently denied read-only commands. Use when the user asks for a doctor run, checkup, audit, tune-up, or cleanup of their Claude Code setup or configuration.","argumentHint":"","aliases":["checkup"],"builtin":true},{"name":"loop","description":"Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). tool instead. It covers the pane's persistent sign-ins, tabs and preview_start, reading pages as text, site approvals, what the pane cannot open, and what to do when it cannot be reached. It is not for Claude in Chrome (mcp__claude-in-chrome__* tools), which has its own skill, and it does not decide which browser to use. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["built-in-browser"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:chrome-browser","description":"Read this skill before the first step that uses Claude in Chrome, the browser extension whose tools are named mcp__claude-in-chrome__* (also called Chrome, the browser extension, or the external browser) and which acts in the person's real Chrome with their own sign-ins; before those tools are turned on there may be a single enable__mcp__claude-in-chrome tool instead. It covers loading the tools in one ToolSearch call, checking the person's open tabs and working in a new tab, site permissions, GIF recordings, console logs, dialogs to avoid, and when to stop and ask. It is not for the built-in browser (mcp__Claude_Browser__* or mcp__remote-devices__Claude_Browser__* tools), which has its own skill, and it does not decide which browser to use. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["chrome-browser"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:computer-use","description":"Read this skill before the first step of any request to do something in an app on the person's own computer (Notes, Finder, System Settings, any desktop app), to look at their screen, or for \"computer use\". Computer use (desktop control) lets Claude take screenshots of the person's desktop and control it with clicks, typing and scrolling through the Claude desktop app; its tools are named mcp__computer-use__* when the session runs in the desktop app and mcp__remote-devices__computer_* when a cloud session is linked to the person's computer; before computer use is turned on for a conversation there may be no such tools, only an enable__mcp__remote-devices__computer tool, which turns it on. It covers turning it on, picking the right tool, the access flow, and the safety rules for tiered apps, links and financial actions. It is not for websites, which go through Claude in Chrome or the built-in browser and their own skills. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["computer-use"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:deep-research","description":"Use this skill when the user's prompt requires (1) researching a topic across multiple sources, comparing options or alternatives, analyzing trends or history, understanding markets or industries, or reviewing literature or studies and (2) synthesizing that research into a comprehensive, narrative report. If you're planning to search the web or internal knowledge bases, consider using this skill. This skill coordinates research subagents, so use it only when you have a tool for spawning subagents (the Agent or Task tool); otherwise, research the question directly. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":""},{"name":"anthropic-skills:docs","description":"docs (living docs people share, comment on and edit; use only when the user asks for one: names a doc, document, page, memo, spec, PRD, runbook or write-up, asks for somewhere to share or keep editing something, or says yes to your doc offer; a plan, comparison, summary or notes asked in chat stays in chat (at most a one-line doc offer); a report, status update, recap or \"something I can send them\" with no form named → ask first: reply, doc or file?; tabs hold tables and live charts too; a pasted claude.ai/code/artifact/… link may be a doc: check with docs tools first; not HTML pages, apps or plain chat answers; a .docx/.pptx/.xlsx/PDF asked for by name → that format's skill): asked for one → no docs-connector instructions in context? call the docs connector's `guide` with topic.instructions first, then create the doc (headings only, no body) before any search, file read or plan, even with files attached. Documenting code means docstrings or repo docs, not a doc. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["docs"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:docx","description":"Use this skill whenever the user wants to create, read, edit, or manipulate Word documents (.docx) or Word templates (.dotx). Triggers include: any mention of 'Word doc', 'word document', '.docx', '.dotx', or requests to produce professional documents with formatting like tables of contents, page numbers, or letterheads. Also use when extracting or reorganizing content from .docx or .dotx files, inserting or replacing images in documents, find-and-replace in Word files, working with tracked changes or comments, or converting content into a polished Word document. If the user asks for a 'report', 'memo', 'letter', 'template', or similar deliverable as a Word or .docx file (to download, email or print), use this skill. However, if they ask for a document, page, report, memo, or notes WITHOUT naming a file format and the session offers Claude's own dedicated document or page skill or connector, use that instead. Do NOT use for PDFs, spreadsheets, Google Docs, or coding unrelated to document generation. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["docx"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:import-memory","description":"Import a memory export from another AI assistant into Claude's memory — conversationally, additively, and with the content treated as data. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["import-memory"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:morning","description":"Render the user's morning brief as a styled HTML artifact, or set it up as a recurring weekday task. Use only when the user explicitly asks to run, see, or set up their morning brief, or if they invoke /morning by name. A question about their day, schedule, or calendar is not by itself a request for the brief; answer it directly instead. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["morning"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:pdf","description":"Use this skill whenever the user wants to do anything with PDF files. This includes reading or extracting text/tables from PDFs, combining or merging multiple PDFs into one, splitting PDFs apart, rotating pages, adding watermarks, creating new PDFs, filling PDF forms, encrypting/decrypting PDFs, extracting images, and OCR on scanned PDFs to make them searchable. If the user mentions a .pdf file or asks to produce one, use this skill. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["pdf"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:pptx","description":"Use this skill any time a .pptx or .potx file is involved in any way — as input, output, or both. This includes: creating slide decks, pitch decks, or presentations as PowerPoint (.pptx) files; reading, parsing, or extracting text from any .pptx or .potx file (even if the extracted content will be used elsewhere, like in an email, summary, or creating a different type of slide deck); editing, modifying, or updating existing presentations; combining or splitting slide files; working with templates (.potx), layouts, speaker notes, or comments. Trigger whenever the user asks for a PowerPoint or .pptx file, or references a .pptx or .potx filename, regardless of what they plan to do with the content afterward. However, when the user asks for a deck, slides, a slide deck, or a presentation without naming a file format, default to using a dedicated slide-deck artifact type or a separate slides skill if this session offers one; otherwise, use this skill. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["pptx"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:setup-writing-style","description":"Learns how the user writes from their own sent messages and docs, and builds a voice profile so future drafts sound like them instead of generic AI. The profile is saved as the my-writing-style skill. Use when the user asks to set up, learn, or capture their writing voice, or complains that drafts sound generic or unlike them and no my-writing-style profile exists. Only for drafting text the user will send as themselves, not for Claude's own replies. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["setup-writing-style"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:skill-creator","description":"Create new skills, modify and improve existing skills, and measure skill performance. Use when users want to create a skill from scratch, edit, or optimize an existing skill, run evals to test a skill, benchmark skill performance with variance analysis, or optimize a skill's description for better triggering accuracy. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["skill-creator"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:xlsx","description":"Use this skill any time a spreadsheet file is the primary input or output. This means any task where the user wants to: open, read, edit, or fix an existing .xlsx, .xlsm, .xltx, .csv, or .tsv file (e.g., adding columns, computing formulas, formatting, charting, cleaning messy data); create a new spreadsheet from scratch or from other data sources; or convert between tabular file formats. Trigger especially when the user references a spreadsheet file by name or path — even casually (like \"the xlsx in my downloads\") — and wants something done to it or produced from it. Also trigger for cleaning or restructuring messy tabular data files (malformed rows, misplaced headers, junk data) into proper spreadsheets. The deliverable must be a spreadsheet file. Do NOT trigger when the primary deliverable is a Word document, HTML report, standalone Python script, database pipeline, or Google Sheets API integration, even if tabular data is involved. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["xlsx"]}],"uuid":"b9619923-7471-4b12-b60e-c7f29b8e3147","session_id":"f5f159c4-ede8-4421-ad05-9b33fc13eec6"}

Promise<IStandardPaginatedResult<EdgeDevice>> {\n568\t    const page = options?.page || 1;\n569\t    const limit = options?.limit || 20;\n570\t    const skip = (page - 1) * limit;\n571\t\n572\t    const where: FindOptionsWhere<EdgeDevice> = { tenantId };\n573\t\n574\t    if (options?.siteId) {\n575\t      where.siteId = options.siteId;\n576\t    }\n577\t    if (options?.lifecycleState) {\n578\t      where.lifecycleState = options.lifecycleState;\n579\t    }\n580\t    if (options?.isOnline !== undefined) {\n581\t      where.isOnline = options.isOnline;\n582\t    }\n583\t    if (options?.search) {\n584\t      // Search by device code or name\n585\t      where.deviceName = ILike(`%${options.search}%`);\n586\t    }\n587\t\n588\t    const [items, total] = await this.deviceRepository.findAndCount({\n589\t      where,\n590\t      order: { createdAt: 'DESC' },\n591\t      skip,\n592\t      take: limit,\n593\t    });\n594\t\n595\t    return createStandardPaginatedResult(items, total, page, limit);\n596\t  }\n597\t\n598\t  /**\n599\t   * Update device information\n600\t   */\n601\t  async updateDevice(\n602\t    id: string,\n603\t    tenantId: string,\n604\t    input: UpdateEdgeDeviceInput,\n605\t  ): Promise<EdgeDevice> {\n606\t    const device = await this.findByIdOrFail(id, tenantId);\n607\t\n608\t    // Apply updates\n609\t    Object.assign(device, input);\n610\t\n611\t    return await this.deviceRepository.save(device);\n612\t  }\n613\t\n614\t  /**\n615\t   * Approve a registered device (move to ACTIVE state)\n616\t   */\n617\t  async approveDevice(id: string, tenantId: string, approvedBy: string): Promise<EdgeDevice> {\n618\t    const device = await this.findByIdOrFail(id, tenantId);\n619\t\n620\t    if (\n621\t      device.lifecycleState !== DeviceLifecycleState.REGISTERED &&\n622\t      device.lifecycleState !== DeviceLifecycleState.PENDING_APPROVAL\n623\t    ) {\n624\t      throw new BadRequestException(\n625\t        `Device in '${device.lifecycleState}' state cannot be approved`,\n626\t      );\n627\t    }\n628\t\n629\t    device.lifecycleState = DeviceLifecycleState.ACTIVE;\n630\t    device.commissionedAt = new Date();\n631\t    device.commissionedBy = approvedBy;\n632\t\n633\t    const saved = await this.deviceRepository.save(device);\n634\t    this.logger.log(`Device approved: ${saved.deviceCode} by ${approvedBy}`);\n635\t    return saved;\n636\t  }\n637\t\n638\t  /**\n639\t   * Set device to maintenance mode\n640\t   */\n641\t  async setMaintenanceMode(id: string, tenantId: string, enabled: boolean): Promise<EdgeDevice> {\n642\t    const device = await this.findByIdOrFail(id, tenantId);\n643\t\n644\t    // SENSOR-MEDIUM-008: REVOKED is terminal too.\n645\t    if (isTerminalLifecycleState(device.lifecycleState)) {\n646\t      throw new BadRequestException(\n647\t        `Cannot change maintenance mode of a device in a terminal state (${device.lifecycleState})`,\n648\t      );\n649\t    }\n650\t\n651\t    device.lifecycleState = enabled\n652\t      ? DeviceLifecycleState.MAINTENANCE\n653\t      : DeviceLifecycleState.ACTIVE;\n654\t\n655\t    return await this.deviceRepository.save(device);\n656\t  }\n657\t\n658\t  /**\n659\t   * Decommission a device\n660\t   */\n661\t  async decommissionDevice(id: string, tenantId: string, reason: string): Promise<EdgeDevice> {\n662\t    const device = await this.findByIdOrFail(id, tenantId);\n663\t\n664\t    device.lifecycleState = DeviceLifecycleState.DECOMMISSIONED;\n665\t    device.isOnline = false;\n666\t    device.config = {\n667\t      ...device.config,\n668\t      decommissionReason: reason,\n669\t      decommissionedAt: new Date().toISOString(),\n670\t    };\n671\t\n672\t    const saved = await this.deviceRepository.save(device);\n673\t    this.logger.log(`Device decommissioned: ${saved.deviceCode}, reason: ${reason}`);\n674\t    return saved;\n675\t  }\n676\t\n677\t  /**\n678\t   * Update device heartbeat (called from MQTT listener)\n679\t   */\n680\t  async updateHeartbeat(heartbeat: DeviceHeartbeat): Promise<EdgeDevice | null> {\n681\t    // Device identifier from MQTT topic can be either deviceCode (e.g. \"PI-A36C09D4\")\n682\t    // or device UUID (e.g. \"0cfb7dad-...\"). The edge agent uses UUID in topic paths.\n683\t    const isUuid = /^[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{12}$/i.test(\n684\t      heartbeat.deviceCode,\n685\t    );\n686\t\n687\t    // Use tenant-scoped query when tenantId is available (MQTT listener always provides it)\n688\t    let device: EdgeDevice | null = null;\n689\t    const tenantSchema = heartbeat.tenantId ? this.getTenantSchemaFromId(heartbeat.tenantId) : null;\n690\t\n691\t    if (tenantSchema) {\n692\t      const column = isUuid ? 'id' : 'device_code';\n693\t      const rows = await this.dataSource.query<RowValueMap[]>(\n694\t        `SELECT * FROM \"${tenantSchema}\".edge_devices WHERE \"${column}\" = $1 LIMIT 1`,\n695\t        [heartbeat.deviceCode],\n696\t      );\n697\t      const firstRow = rows[0];\n698\t      if (firstRow) {\n699\t        device = this.mapRowToEdgeDevice(firstRow);\n700\t      }\n701\t    } else {\n702\t      const whereCondition: Record<string, unknown> = isUuid\n703\t        ? { id: heartbeat.deviceCode }\n704\t        : { deviceCode: heartbeat.deviceCode };\n705\t      device = await this.deviceRepository.findOne({ where: whereCondition });\n706\t    }\n707\t\n708\t    if (!device) {\n709\t      this.logger.warn(`Heartbeat from unknown device: ${heartbeat.deviceCode}`);\n710\t      return null;\n711\t    }\n712\t\n713\t    // Update device health metrics\n714\t    device.lastSeenAt = new Date();\n715\t    device.isOnline = heartbeat.isOnline;\n716\t\n717\t    if (heartbeat.cpuUsage !== undefined) device.cpuUsage = heartbeat.cpuUsage;\n718\t    if (heartbeat.memoryUsage !== undefined) device.memoryUsage = heartbeat.memoryUsage;\n719\t    if (heartbeat.storageUsage !== undefined) device.storageUsage = heartbeat.storageUsage;\n720\t    if (heartbeat.temperatureCelsius !== undefined)\n721\t      device.temperatureCelsius = heartbeat.temperatureCelsius;\n722\t    if (heartbeat.uptimeSeconds !== undefined) device.uptimeSeconds = heartbeat.uptimeSeconds;\n723\t    if (heartbeat.firmwareVersion) {\n724\t      device.firmwareVersion = heartbeat.firmwareVersion;\n725\t\n726\t      // Check if firmware update target has been reached\n727\t      if (device.targetFirmwareVersion) {\n728\t        const normalize = (v: string): string => v.replace(/^agent-v/, '');\n729\t        if (normalize(heartbeat.firmwareVersion) === normalize(device.targetFirmwareVersion)) {\n730\t          device.firmwareUpdatedAt = new Date();\n731\t          device.targetFirmwareVersion = undefined;\n732\t          this.logger.log(\n733\t            `Device ${device.deviceCode} firmware updated to ${heartbeat.firmwareVersion}`,\n734\t          );\n735\t        }\n736\t      }\n737\t    }\n738\t    if (heartbeat.ipAddress) device.ipAddress = heartbeat.ipAddress;\n739\t\n740\t    // Update connection quality based on frequency of heartbeats\n741\t    device.connectionQuality = heartbeat.isOnline ? 100 : 0;\n742\t\n743\t    // Map status string to lifecycle state (skip DECOMMISSIONED/REVOKED — those are admin-only)\n744\t    const status = heartbeat.status;\n745\t    if (status === 'error') {\n746\t      device.lifecycleState = DeviceLifecycleState.ERROR;\n747\t    } else if (status === 'maintenance') {\n748\t      device.lifecycleState = DeviceLifecycleState.MAINTENANCE;\n749\t    } else if (status === 'offline' && device.lifecycleState === DeviceLifecycleState.ACTIVE) {\n750\t      device.lifecycleState = DeviceLifecycleState.OFFLINE;\n751\t    } else if (\n752\t      heartbeat.isOnline &&\n753\t      (device.lifecycleState === DeviceLifecycleState.OFFLINE ||\n754\t        device.lifecycleState === DeviceLifecycleState.PROVISIONING)\n755\t    ) {\n756\t      // Transition to ACTIVE when device comes online from OFFLINE or PROVISIONING state\n757\t      device.lifecycleState = DeviceLifecycleState.ACTIVE;\n758\t    } else if (!heartbeat.isOnline && device.lifecycleState === DeviceLifecycleState.ACTIVE) {\n759\t      // Transition to OFFLINE when device goes offline while in ACTIVE state\n760\t      device.lifecycleState = DeviceLifecycleState.OFFLINE;\n761\t    }\n762\t\n763\t    // Save using tenant-scoped query when available\n764\t    if (tenantSchema) {\n765\t      await this.dataSource.query(\n766\t        `UPDATE \"${tenantSchema}\".edge_devices SET\n767\t          last_seen_at = $2, is_online = $3, lifecycle_state = $4,\n768\t          cpu_usage = COALESCE($5, cpu_usage), memory_usage = COALESCE($6, memory_usage),\n769\t          storage_usage = COALESCE($7, storage_usage), temperature_celsius = COALESCE($8, temperature_celsius),\n770\t          uptime_seconds = COALESCE($9, uptime_seconds), firmware_version = COALESCE($10, firmware_version),\n771\t          ip_address = COALESCE($11, ip_address), connection_quality = $12,\n772\t          updated_at = NOW()\n773\t        WHERE id = $1`,\n774\t        [\n775\t          device.id,\n776\t          device.lastSeenAt,\n777\t      siteId?: string;\n      lifecycleState?: DeviceLifecycleState;\n      isOnline?: boolean;\n      search?: string;\n      page?: number;\n      limit?: number;\n    },\n  ): Promise<IStandardPaginatedResult<EdgeDevice>> {\n    const page = options?.page || 1;\n    const limit = options?.limit || 20;\n    const skip = (page - 1) * limit;\n\n    const where: FindOptionsWhere<EdgeDevice> = { tenantId };\n\n    if (options?.siteId) {\n      where.siteId = options.siteId;\n    }\n    if (options?.lifecycleState) {\n      where.lifecycleState = options.lifecycleState;\n    }\n    if (options?.isOnline !== undefined) {\n      where.isOnline = options.isOnline;\n    }\n    if (options?.search) {\n      // Search by device code or name\n      where.deviceName = ILike(`%${options.search}%`);\n    }\n\n    const [items, total] = await this.deviceRepository.findAndCount({\n      where,\n      order: { createdAt: 'DESC' },\n      skip,\n      take: limit,\n    });\n\n    return createStandardPaginatedResult(items, total, page, limit);\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Update device information\n   */\n  async updateDevice(\n    id: string,\n    tenantId: string,\n    input: UpdateEdgeDeviceInput,\n  ): Promise<EdgeDevice> {\n    const device = await this.findByIdOrFail(id, tenantId);\n\n    // Apply updates\n    Object.assign(device, input);\n\n    return await this.deviceRepository.save(device);\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Approve a registered device (move to ACTIVE state)\n   */\n  async approveDevice(id: string, tenantId: string, approvedBy: string): Promise<EdgeDevice> {\n    const device = await this.findByIdOrFail(id, tenantId);\n\n    if (\n      device.lifecycleState !== DeviceLifecycleState.REGISTERED &&\n      device.lifecycleState !== DeviceLifecycleState.PENDING_APPROVAL\n    ) {\n      throw new BadRequestException(\n        `Device in '${device.lifecycleState}' state cannot be approved`,\n      );\n    }\n\n    device.lifecycleState = DeviceLifecycleState.ACTIVE;\n    device.commissionedAt = new Date();\n    device.commissionedBy = approvedBy;\n\n    const saved = await this.deviceRepository.save(device);\n    this.logger.log(`Device approved: ${saved.deviceCode} by ${approvedBy}`);\n    return saved;\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Set device to maintenance mode\n   */\n  async setMaintenanceMode(id: string, tenantId: string, enabled: boolean): Promise<EdgeDevice> {\n    const device = await this.findByIdOrFail(id, tenantId);\n\n    // SENSOR-MEDIUM-008: REVOKED is terminal too.\n    if (isTerminalLifecycleState(device.lifecycleState)) {\n      throw new BadRequestException(\n        `Cannot change maintenance mode of a device in a terminal state (${device.lifecycleState})`,\n      );\n    }\n\n    device.lifecycleState = enabled\n      ? DeviceLifecycleState.MAINTENANCE\n      : DeviceLifecycleState.ACTIVE;\n\n    return await this.deviceRepository.save(device);\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Decommission a device\n   */\n  async decommissionDevice(id: string, tenantId: string, reason: string): Promise<EdgeDevice> {\n    const device = await this.findByIdOrFail(id, tenantId);\n\n    device.lifecycleState = DeviceLifecycleState.DECOMMISSIONED;\n    device.isOnline = false;\n    device.config = {\n      ...device.config,\n      decommissionReason: reason,\n      decommissionedAt: new Date().toISOString(),\n    };\n\n    const saved = await this.deviceRepository.save(device);\n    this.logger.log(`Device decommissioned: ${saved.deviceCode}, reason: ${reason}`);\n    return saved;\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Update device heartbeat (called from MQTT listener)\n   */\n  async updateHeartbeat(heartbeat: DeviceHeartbeat): Promise<EdgeDevice | null> {\n    // Device identifier from MQTT topic can be either deviceCode (e.g. \"PI-A36C09D4\")\n    // or device UUID (e.g. \"0cfb7dad-...\"). The edge agent uses UUID in topic paths.\n    const isUuid = /^[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{12}$/i.test(\n      heartbeat.deviceCode,\n    );\n\n    // Use tenant-scoped query when tenantId is available (MQTT listener always provides it)\n    let device: EdgeDevice | null = null;\n    const tenantSchema = heartbeat.tenantId ? this.getTenantSchemaFromId(heartbeat.tenantId) : null;\n\n    if (tenantSchema) {\n      const column = isUuid ? 'id' : 'device_code';\n      const rows = await this.dataSource.query<RowValueMap[]>(\n        `SELECT * FROM \"${tenantSchema}\".edge_devices WHERE \"${column}\" = $1 LIMIT 1`,\n        [heartbeat.deviceCode],\n      );\n      const firstRow = rows[0];\n      if (firstRow) {\n        device = this.mapRowToEdgeDevice(firstRow);\n      }\n    } else {\n      const whereCondition: Record<string, unknown> = isUuid\n        ? { id: heartbeat.deviceCode }\n        : { deviceCode: heartbeat.deviceCode };\n      device = await this.deviceRepository.findOne({ where: whereCondition });\n    }\n\n    if (!device) {\n      this.logger.warn(`Heartbeat from unknown device: ${heartbeat.deviceCode}`);\n      return null;\n    }\n\n    // Update device health metrics\n    device.lastSeenAt = new Date();\n    device.isOnline = heartbeat.isOnline;\n\n    if (heartbeat.cpuUsage !== undefined) device.cpuUsage = heartbeat.cpuUsage;\n    if (heartbeat.memoryUsage !== undefined) device.memoryUsage = heartbeat.memoryUsage;\n    if (heartbeat.storageUsage !== undefined) device.storageUsage = heartbeat.storageUsage;\n    if (heartbeat.temperatureCelsius !== undefined)\n      device.temperatureCelsius = heartbeat.temperatureCelsius;\n    if (heartbeat.uptimeSeconds !== undefined) device.uptimeSeconds = heartbeat.uptimeSeconds;\n    if (heartbeat.firmwareVersion) {\n      device.firmwareVersion = heartbeat.firmwareVersion;\n\n      // Check if firmware update target has been reached\n      if (device.targetFirmwareVersion) {\n        const normalize = (v: string): string => v.replace(/^agent-v/, '');\n        if (normalize(heartbeat.firmwareVersion) === normalize(device.targetFirmwareVersion)) {\n          device.firmwareUpdatedAt = new Date();\n          device.targetFirmwareVersion = undefined;\n          this.logger.log(\n            `Device ${device.deviceCode} firmware updated to ${heartbeat.firmwareVersion}`,\n          );\n        }\n      }\n    }\n    if (heartbeat.ipAddress) device.ipAddress = heartbeat.ipAddress;\n\n    // Update connection quality based on frequency of heartbeats\n    device.connectionQuality = heartbeat.isOnline ? 100 : 0;\n\n    // Map status string to lifecycle state (skip DECOMMISSIONED/REVOKED — those are admin-only)\n    const status = heartbeat.status;\n    if (status === 'error') {\n      device.lifecycleState = DeviceLifecycleState.ERROR;\n    } else if (status === 'maintenance') {\n      device.lifecycleState = DeviceLifecycleState.MAINTENANCE;\n    } else if (status === 'offline' && device.lifecycleState === DeviceLifecycleState.ACTIVE) {\n      device.lifecycleState = DeviceLifecycleState.OFFLINE;\n    } else if (\n      heartbeat.isOnline &&\n      (device.lifecycleState === DeviceLifecycleState.OFFLINE ||\n        device.lifecycleState === DeviceLifecycleState.PROVISIONING)\n    ) {\n      // Transition to ACTIVE when device comes online from OFFLINE or PROVISIONING state\n      device.lifecycleState = DeviceLifecycleState.ACTIVE;\n    } else if (!heartbeat.isOnline && device.lifecycleState === DeviceLifecycleState.ACTIVE) {\n      // Transition to OFFLINE when device goes offline while in ACTIVE state\n      device.lifecycleState = DeviceLifecycleState.OFFLINE;\n    }\n\n    // Save using tenant-scoped query when available\n    if (tenantSchema) {\n      await this.dataSource.query(\n        `UPDATE \"${tenantSchema}\".edge_devices SET\n          last_seen_at = $2, is_online = $3, lifecycle_state = $4,\n          cpu_usage = COALESCE($5, cpu_usage), memory_usage = COALESCE($6, memory_usage),\n          storage_usage = COALESCE($7, storage_usage), temperature_celsius = COALESCE($8, temperature_celsius),\n          uptime_seconds = COALESCE($9, uptime_seconds), firmware_version = COALESCE($10, firmware_version),\n          ip_address = COALESCE($11, ip_address), connection_quality = $12,\n          updated_at = NOW()\n        WHERE id = $1`,\n        [\n          device.id,\n          device.lastSeenAt,\n          device.isOnline,\n          device.lifecycleState,\n          device.cpuUsage ?? null,\n          device.memoryUsage ?? null,\n          device.storageUsage ?? null,\n          device.temperatureCelsius ?? null,\n          device.uptimeSeconds ?? null,\n          device.firmwareVersion ?? null,\n          device.ipAddress ?? null,\n          device.connectionQuality,\n        ],\n      );\n      return device;\n    }\n\n    // SAFETY: Block writes without tenant context — would contaminate source schema\n    this.logger.error(\n      `BLOCKED: updateHeartbeat for device ${device.deviceCode ?? device.id} has no tenantSchema — would write to source schema`,\n    );\n    return device;\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Mark devices as offline if no heartbeat received.\n   *\n   * Iterates ALL tenant schemas so that devices in every tenant are checked,\n   * not just the default search_path (which would only hit the `sensor` schema).\n   */\n  /**\n   * The scheduled entry point. Separate from the method below because a\n   * scheduled tick takes no arguments and returns nothing — the timeout is a\n   * parameter of the operation, not of the schedule, and the affected count is\n   * a result for a caller that asked.\n   */\n  @ScheduledJob({ name: 'edge-device.mark-stale-offline', every: 60_000 })\n  async markStaleDevicesOfflineTick(): Promise<void> {\n    await this.markStaleDevicesOffline();\n  }\n\n  async markStaleDevicesOffline(timeoutMinutes = 5): Promise<number> {\n    const cutoff = new Date(Date.now() - timeoutMinutes * 60 * 1000);\n    let totalAffected = 0;\n","numLines":260,"startLine":560,"totalLines":2745}}}

Empty object = nothing dropped.\n      observations_truncated: truncated,\n    },\n  };\n}\n\nfunction analyzeTenantUnit(\n  unit: SourceUnit,\n  result: AnalysisResult,\n  allowlist: ReadonlySet<string>,\n  context: AnalysisContext,\n): void {\n  const allowlisted = allowlist.has(unit.relativePath);\n  const hasTenantSignal = /\\btenantId\\b|@Tenant\\b|TenantGuard|tenantManagerRepo|TenantContext/.test(unit.text);\n  const guardNames = collectIdentifiers(unit.sourceFile).filter((name) => TENANT_GUARD_NAMES.has(name));\n\n  if (hasTenantSignal) {\n    result.observations.push({\n      id: `tenant-source:${unit.relativePath}`,\n      type: 'tenant_source',\n      path: unit.relativePath,\n      line: 1,\n      details: {\n        hasTenantIdReference: /\\btenantId\\b/.test(unit.text),\n        hasTenantDecorator: /@Tenant\\b/.test(unit.text),\n        hasTenantManagerRepo: /tenantManagerRepo/.test(unit.text),\n        guardNames,\n      },\n    });\n  }\n\n  visit(unit.sourceFile, (node) => {\n    if (!ts.isCallExpression(node) || !ts.isPropertyAccessExpression(node.expression)) {\n      return;\n    }\n    const method = node.expression.name.text;\n    if (!REPOSITORY_METHODS.has(method) && !RAW_QUERY_METHODS.has(method)) {\n      return;\n    }\n    const line = lineOf(unit.sourceFile, node);\n    const enclosing = enclosingFunctionLike(node);\n    const scopeText = enclosing?.getText(unit.sourceFile) ?? unit.sourceFile.getText();\n    const callText = node.getText(unit.sourceFile);\n    const statementText = enclosingStatementText(node, unit.sourceFile);\n    const receiverText = node.expression.expression.getText(unit.sourceFile);\n    const boundary = classifyTenantBoundaryCall(node, unit, context);\n    if (!boundary.isBoundary) {\n      return;\n    }\n    const scopedByTenantManager = /tenantManagerRepo|tenantAware|tenantScoped|withTenant/i.test(receiverText + scopeText);\n    const callHasTenantPredicate = /\\btenantId\\b|tenant_id|current_tenant|set_config\\(['\"]app\\.current_tenant/.test(callText + statementText);\n    const scopeHasTenant = /\\btenantId\\b|@Tenant\\b|TenantContext|tenantManagerRepo/.test(scopeText);\n    const explicitCrossTenant = hasCrossTenantComment(unit.text, node.getFullStart(), node.getStart(unit.sourceFile));\n\n    result.observations.push({\n      id: `tenant-repository-call:${unit.relativePath}:${line}:${method}`,\n      type: 'tenant_repository_call',\n      path: unit.relativePath,\n      line,\n      name: method,\n      details: {\n        receiver: receiverText,\n        scopeHasTenant,\n        callHasTenantPredicate,\n        scopedByTenantManager,\n        explicitCrossTenant,\n        allowlisted,\n        boundaryKind: boundary.kind,\n        entityName: boundary.entityName ?? null,\n        typeEvidence: boundary.typeEvidence,\n      },\n    });\n\n    if (\n      allowlisted\n      || explicitCrossTenant\n      || scopedByTenantManager\n      || unit.relativePath.includes('/migrations/')\n      || !scopeHasTenant\n      || callHasTenantPredicate\n      || !boundary.tenantOwned\n    ) {\n      return;\n    }\n    if (RAW_QUERY_METHODS.has(method)) {\n      result.findings.push({\n        id: `tenant-raw-query-missing-predicate:${unit.relativePath}:${line}`,\n        rule: 'tenant_raw_query_missing_tenant_predicate',\n        severity: 'high',\n        path: unit.relativePath,\n        line,\n        message: 'Raw tenant-bound query is executed in a tenant-aware scope without an explicit tenant predicate.',\n        evidence: [{ path: unit.relativePath, line }],\n        confidence: isSeedOrBootstrapContext(unit.relativePath, scopeText) ? 0.55 : 0.9,\n        actionability: isSeedOrBootstrapContext(unit.relativePath, scopeText) ? 'review_required' : 'actionable',\n        review_reason: isSeedOrBootstrapContext(unit.relativePath, scopeText) ? 'seed_or_bootstrap_context' : undefined,\n        details: { method, receiver: receiverText, entityName: boundary.entityName ?? null },\n      });\n      return;\n    }\n    if (!inputAllowsRepositoryReadFinding(context, method)) {\n      return;\n    }\n    result.findings.push({\n      id: `tenant-repository-unscoped-read:${unit.relativePath}:${line}`,\n      rule: 'tenant_repository_unscoped_read',\n      severity: method === 'find' || method === 'findOne' || method === 'findOneBy' || method === 'findBy' ? 'medium' : 'high',\n      path: unit.relativePath,\n      line,\n      message: 'Repository call is made in a tenant-aware scope without an explicit tenant predicate or tenant-scoped repository helper.',\n      evidence: [{ path: unit.relativePath, line }],\n      confidence: 0.82,\n      actionability: 'actionable',\n      details: { method, receiver: receiverText, entityName: boundary.entityName ?? null },\n    });\n  });\n}\n\nfunction enclosingStatementText(node: ts.Node, sourceFile: ts.SourceFile): string {\n  let current: ts.Node | undefined = node;\n  while (current && !ts.isStatement(current)) {\n    current = current.parent;\n  }\n  return current?.getText(sourceFile) ?? node.getText(sourceFile);\n}\n\nfunction isSeedOrBootstrapContext(path: string, text: string): boolean {\n  return /seed|bootstrap|fixture/i.test(path) || /\\b(seed|bootstrap|fixture)\\b/i.test(text.slice(0, 4000));\n}","numLines":130,"startLine":180,"totalLines":574}}}

Math.round(m.uptime_secs) : m.uptimeSeconds,\n      firmwareVersion: m.agent_version ?? m.firmwareVersion ?? payload.agent_version,\n      ipAddress: m.ip_address ?? m.ipAddress,\n    };\n\n    if (!this.edgeDeviceService) return;\n    const device = await this.edgeDeviceService.updateHeartbeat(heartbeat);\n","numLines":30,"startLine":1244,"totalLines":2427}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01S4X1rCZVQpVsuMPY2AgfMs","type":"tool_result","content":"460:def _check_agent_ref(\n461-    ref: str,\n462-    *,\n463-    root: Path,\n464-    errors: list[dict[str, Any]],\n465-    checked: list[str],\n466-    allow_self_output: bool = False,\n467-    allow_kernel_artifacts: bool = False,\n468-) -> None:\n469-    # Y7 follow-through (ORPHAN-708) — the kernel's OWN adjudication mint\n470-    # (human_required_adjudication.open_adjudication) issues\n471-    # ``human-required:<request-id>`` refs, and the first real panel drain\n472-    # showed this validator rejecting them: mint-side and law-side of the\n473-    # same kernel disagreed, every panel opinion died submit_rejected, and\n474-    # the rejection BURNED the envelope's requeue budget. The ref is a\n475-    # LEDGER POINTER, not a repo path — the load-bearing verification\n476-    # happens at fold time (fold_adjudication / adjudicate_human_required\n477-    # resolve the record from the store before any disposition acts), so\n478-    # the law admits the pointer as checked rather than pretending a repo\n479-    # check that cannot exist for state-store records.\n480-    if _is_ledger_pointer_ref(ref):\n481-        checked.append(ref)\n482-        return\n483-    if allow_kernel_artifacts:\n484-        admissible, reason = _kernel_artifact_verdict(ref, root)\n485-        if admissible:\n486-            checked.append(ref)\n487-            return\n488-        if reason != \"artifact_not_recorded_in_results_ledger\":\n489-            # It IS an artifact path — just not one the ledger vouches for.\n490-            # Naming the failure is the point: a silent fall-through to the\n491-            # repo check would report \"not repo verified\" about a file that\n492-            # was never meant to be in the repo.\n493-            errors.append({\n494-                \"code\": \"agent_evidence_artifact_unverifiable\",\n495-                \"ref\": ref,\n496-                \"reason\": reason,\n497-            })\n498-            return\n499-    parsed = _parse_agent_ref(ref)\n500-    if parsed is None:\n501-        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_ref_malformed\", \"ref\": ref})\n502-        return\n503-    path, line = parsed\n504-    # Plan 024 v3 §H-5 — canonical-resolve BEFORE the SELF_OUTPUT\n505-    # prefix check. Pre-fix the prefix match operated on the\n506-    # lexically-normalized string; a `src/../aria-tools/...` traversal\n507-    # could bypass detection if normalize_path didn't fully collapse\n508-    # it. Post-fix the resolution runs first, then SELF_OUTPUT is\n509-    # decided on the canonical posix-relative form.\n510-    from .tool_registry import GovernanceError as _GE\n511-    try:\n512-        rel_str, absolute = _canonical_evidence_path(path, root)\n513-    except _GE as exc:\n514-        msg = str(exc)\n515-        if msg.startswith(\"evidence_path_outside_repo\"):\n516-            errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_path_escapes_workspace\", \"path\": path})\n517-        else:\n518-            errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_path_unresolvable\", \"path\": path,\n519-                           \"error\": msg})\n520-        return"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"f5f159c4-ede8-4421-ad05-9b33fc13eec6","uuid":"7db7172d-b058-4322-a02e-371fdbd9c913","timestamp":"2026-09-19T14:06:04.144Z","tool_use_result":{"mode":"content","numFiles":0,"filenames":[],"content":"460:def _check_agent_ref(\n461-    ref: str,\n462-    *,\n463-    root: Path,\n464-    errors: list[dict[str, Any]],\n465-    checked: list[str],\n466-    allow_self_output: bool = False,\n467-    allow_kernel_artifacts: bool = False,\n468-) -> None:\n469-    # Y7 follow-through (ORPHAN-708) — the kernel's OWN adjudication mint\n470-    # (human_required_adjudication.open_adjudication) issues\n471-    # ``human-required:<request-id>`` refs, and the first real panel drain\n472-    # showed this validator rejecting them: mint-side and law-side of the\n473-    # same kernel disagreed, every panel opinion died submit_rejected, and\n474-    # the rejection BURNED the envelope's requeue budget. The ref is a\n475-    # LEDGER POINTER, not a repo path — the load-bearing verification\n476-    # happens at fold time (fold_adjudication / adjudicate_human_required\n477-    # resolve the record from the store before any disposition acts), so\n478-    # the law admits the pointer as checked rather than pretending a repo\n479-    # check that cannot exist for state-store records.\n480-    if _is_ledger_pointer_ref(ref):\n481-        checked.append(ref)\n482-        return\n483-    if allow_kernel_artifacts:\n484-        admissible, reason = _kernel_artifact_verdict(ref, root)\n485-        if admissible:\n486-            checked.append(ref)\n487-            return\n488-        if reason != \"artifact_not_recorded_in_results_ledger\":\n489-            # It IS an artifact path — just not one the ledger vouches for.\n490-            # Naming the failure is the point: a silent fall-through to the\n491-            # repo check would report \"not repo verified\" about a file that\n492-            # was never meant to be in the repo.\n493-            errors.append({\n494-                \"code\": \"agent_evidence_artifact_unverifiable\",\n495-                \"ref\": ref,\n496-                \"reason\": reason,\n497-            })\n498-            return\n499-    parsed = _parse_agent_ref(ref)\n500-    if parsed is None:\n501-        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_ref_malformed\", \"ref\": ref})\n502-        return\n503-    path, line = parsed\n504-    # Plan 024 v3 §H-5 — canonical-resolve BEFORE the SELF_OUTPUT\n505-    # prefix check. Pre-fix the prefix match operated on the\n506-    # lexically-normalized string; a `src/../aria-tools/...` traversal\n507-    # could bypass detection if normalize_path didn't fully collapse\n508-    # it. Post-fix the resolution runs first, then SELF_OUTPUT is\n509-    # decided on the canonical posix-relative form.\n510-    from .tool_registry import GovernanceError as _GE\n511-    try:\n512-        rel_str, absolute = _canonical_evidence_path(path, root)\n513-    except _GE as exc:\n514-        msg = str(exc)\n515-        if msg.startswith(\"evidence_path_outside_repo\"):\n516-            errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_path_escapes_workspace\", \"path\": path})\n517-        else:\n518-            errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_path_unresolvable\", \"path\": path,\n519-                           \"error\": msg})\n520-        return","numLines":61,"totalLines":61}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_014RKW5rf9WBhsA3yqHLE5KS","type":"tool_result","content":"520\t        return\n521\t    # ARIA self-output / runtime artefacts are NOT admissible evidence\n522\t    # for an agent claim (CLAUDE.md L1, Plan 016 §Agent output untrusted).\n523\t    if not allow_self_output and rel_str.startswith(SELF_OUTPUT_MARKERS):\n524\t        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_self_output\", \"path\": rel_str})\n525\t        return\n526\t    if not absolute.exists() or not absolute.is_file():\n527\t        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_path_missing\", \"path\": path})\n528\t        return\n529\t    checked.append(rel_str)\n530\t    if line is None:\n531\t        return\n532\t    if line <= 0:\n533\t        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_line_invalid\", \"path\": path, \"line\": line})\n534\t        return\n535\t    line_count = len(absolute.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\", errors=\"replace\").splitlines())\n536\t    if line > line_count:\n537\t        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_line_missing\", \"path\": path, \"line\": line, \"line_count\": line_count})\n538\t\n539\t\n540\tdef validate_agent_response_evidence(\n541\t    *,\n542\t    response: dict[str, Any],\n543\t    workspace_root: str | Path,\n544\t    request: dict[str, Any] | None = None,\n545\t    probe_session: GitProbeSession | None = None,\n546\t) -> dict[str, Any]:\n547\t    \"\"\"Re-fetch every evidence ref claimed by an agent response and verify it exists in the repo.\n548\t\n549\t    Why: Plan 016 V-24 — agent output is data, not truth. The contract\n550\t    layer (agent_contract.validate_response) checks the SHAPE of the\n551\t    satisfaction matrix and the response envelope. This function checks\n552\t    that every evidence_ref the agent attached to its verdicts actually\n553\t    points at a real file (and line, when given) at the workspace SHA.\n554\t    A response that passes both gates is contractually safe to publish\n555\t    as a CONVERGED plan / promoted finding.\n556\t\n557\t    Returns: {\n558\t        \"valid\": bool,\n559\t        \"errors\": [{\"code\": ..., \"path\": ..., ...}],"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"f5f159c4-ede8-4421-ad05-9b33fc13eec6","uuid":"48a229eb-99fd-46ff-9a05-ca48b6b57fc3","timestamp":"2026-09-19T14:06:07.737Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-0db60b150821/aria-kernel/aria_kernel/evidence_validator.py","content":"        return\n    # ARIA self-output / runtime artefacts are NOT admissible evidence\n    # for an agent claim (CLAUDE.md L1, Plan 016 §Agent output untrusted).\n    if not allow_self_output and rel_str.startswith(SELF_OUTPUT_MARKERS):\n        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_self_output\", \"path\": rel_str})\n        return\n    if not absolute.exists() or not absolute.is_file():\n        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_path_missing\", \"path\": path})\n        return\n    checked.append(rel_str)\n    if line is None:\n        return\n    if line <= 0:\n        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_line_invalid\", \"path\": path, \"line\": line})\n        return\n    line_count = len(absolute.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\", errors=\"replace\").splitlines())\n    if line > line_count:\n        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_line_missing\", \"path\": path, \"line\": line, \"line_count\": line_count})\n\n\ndef validate_agent_response_evidence(\n    *,\n    response: dict[str, Any],\n    workspace_root: str | Path,\n    request: dict[str, Any] | None = None,\n    probe_session: GitProbeSession | None = None,\n) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Re-fetch every evidence ref claimed by an agent response and verify it exists in the repo.\n\n    Why: Plan 016 V-24 — agent output is data, not truth. The contract\n    layer (agent_contract.validate_response) checks the SHAPE of the\n    satisfaction matrix and the response envelope. This function checks\n    that every evidence_ref the agent attached to its verdicts actually\n    points at a real file (and line, when given) at the workspace SHA.\n    A response that passes both gates is contractually safe to publish\n    as a CONVERGED plan / promoted finding.\n\n    Returns: {\n        \"valid\": bool,\n        \"errors\": [{\"code\": ..., \"path\": ..., ...}],","numLines":40,"startLine":520,"totalLines":774}}}

The raw query at edge-device.service.ts:693-696 reads `\\\"${tenantSchema}\\\".edge_devices` and is reachable only inside `if (tenantSchema)` (line 691), where tenantSchema is derived from heartbeat.tenantId (line 689) via getTenantSchemaName (lines 2591-2593; libs/backend-common/src/database/tenant-schema.utils.ts:76-79). edge_devices is a per-tenant table (`@Entity('edge_devices')` with no schema: at edge-device.entity.ts:109), so each tenant's rows exist only inside that tenant's own schema and the schema-qualified table reference is the tenant boundary of this query. The adapter fired because callHasTenantPredicate (tools/aria-adapters/tenant-scoping-adapter.ts:231) is a text regex that recognises only tenantId | tenant_id | current_tenant | set_config('app.current_tenant and has no notion of a tenant-schema qualifier, while scopeHasTenant (line 232) is true from line 689. Detector recognition gap, not a missing tenant predicate.\",\n      \"evidence_refs\": [\n        \"apps/sensor-service/src/edge-device/edge-device.service.ts:689\",\n \"finding_id\": \"tenant-raw-query-missing-predicate:apps/sensor-service/src/edge-device/edge-device.service.ts:693\",\n      \"rule\": \"tenant_raw_query_missing_tenant_predicate\",\n      \"verdict\": \"false_positive\",\n      \"judge_id\": \"aria-evidence-judge\",\n      \"model\": \"claude-opus-5\",\n      \"prompt_hash\": null,\n      \"confidence\": 0.88,\n      \"rationale\": \"The flagged call (apps/sensor-service/src/edge-device/edge-device.service.ts:693-696) executes `SELECT * FROM \\\"${tenantSchema}\\\".edge_devices WHERE \\\"${column}\\\" = $1 LIMIT 1` with [heartbeat.deviceCode]. It is reachable only inside `if (tenantSchema)` (line 691); tenantSchema is `heartbeat.tenantId ? this.getTenantSchemaFromId(heartbeat.tenantId) : null` (line 689), and getTenantSchemaFromId delegates to the shared getTenantSchemaName (lines 2591-2593), which renders `tenant_<first 16 hex of the uuid>` (libs/backend-common/src/database/tenant-schema.utils.ts:76-79). edge_devices is a per-tenant table: `@Entity('edge_devices')` declares no `schema:` (apps/sensor-service/src/edge-device/entities/edge-device.entity.ts:109), so under the schema-per-tenant model (ADR-011, root CLAUDE.md CRITICAL block) each tenant's rows exist only inside that tenant's own schema. The schema-qualified table reference is therefore the tenant boundary of this query: it cannot return a row belonging to any tenant other than heartbeat.tenantId. The entity does carry a tenant_id column (edge-device.entity.ts:123), but a `tenant_id = $2` predicate bound to the same heartbeat.tenantId would duplicate the qualifier rather than add an isolation boundary, so the HIGH cross-tenant-exposure claim the rule message makes does not hold at this line. Why the adapter fired: callHasTenantPredicate at tools/aria-adapters/tenant-scoping-adapter.ts:231 is a regex over the call and statement text that recognises only `tenantId`, `tenant_id`, `current_tenant` and `set_config('app.current_tenant`; the statement contains `tenantSchema` and `heartbeat.deviceCode`, so the regex misses the schema qualifier, while scopeHasTenant (line 232) is true because the enclosing function references heartbeat.tenantId at line 689; the gate at lines 254-268 then emits the finding. A grep of the adapter for tenantSchema / schema-qualified handling returns nothing, so the detector has no concept of a schema qualifier as a tenant predicate. The sibling UPDATE in the same function (lines 765-773, `UPDATE \\\"${tenantSchema}\\\".edge_devices ... WHERE id = $1`) follows the same schema-qualified convention. The adapter's own fixture already records judge-diagnosed false-positive classes for this rule (semantic-raw-query-predicates.json:19, `predicated_raw_query`); this finding is a sibling class, schema-qualified raw query. Root-cause change in the detector (Tier 3, make the correct behaviour detectable): extend callHasTenantPredicate at tenant-scoping-adapter.ts:231 to recognise a tenant-schema-qualified table reference (an interpolated identifier derived from getTenantSchemaName / tenantSchema in the statement text) and add a `schema_qualified_raw_query` false_positive_class with a fixture case, so the rule stops labelling correctly isolated schema-per-tenant queries as HIGH leaks.\",\n      \"evidence_refs\": [\n        \"apps/sensor-service/src/edge-device/edge-device.service.ts:689\",\n \"The flagged call (apps/sensor-service/src/edge-device/edge-device.service.ts:693-696) executes `SELECT * FROM \\\"${tenantSchema}\\\".edge_devices WHERE \\\"${column}\\\" = $1 LIMIT 1` with [heartbeat.deviceCode]. It is reachable only inside `if (tenantSchema)` (line 691); tenantSchema is `heartbeat.tenantId ? this.getTenantSchemaFromId(heartbeat.tenantId) : null` (line 689), and getTenantSchemaFromId delegates to the shared getTenantSchemaName (lines 2591-2593), which renders `tenant_<first 16 hex of the uuid>` (libs/backend-common/src/database/tenant-schema.utils.ts:76-79). edge_devices is a per-tenant table: `@Entity('edge_devices')` declares no `schema:` (apps/sensor-service/src/edge-device/entities/edge-device.entity.ts:109), so under the schema-per-tenant model (ADR-011, root CLAUDE.md CRITICAL block) each tenant's rows exist only inside that tenant's own schema. The schema-qualified table reference is therefore the tenant boundary of this query: it cannot return a row belonging to any tenant other than heartbeat.tenantId. The entity does carry a tenant_id column (edge-device.entity.ts:123), but a `tenant_id = $2` predicate bound to the same heartbeat.tenantId would duplicate the qualifier rather than add an isolation boundary, so the HIGH cross-tenant-exposure claim the rule message makes does not hold at this line. Why the adapter fired: callHasTenantPredicate at tools/aria-adapters/tenant-scoping-adapter.ts:231 is a regex over the call and statement text that recognises only `tenantId`, `tenant_id`, `current_tenant` and `set_config('app.current_tenant`; the statement contains `tenantSchema` and `heartbeat.deviceCode`, so the regex misses the schema qualifier, while scopeHasTenant (line 232) is true because the enclosing function references heartbeat.tenantId at line 689; the gate at lines 254-268 then emits the finding. A grep of the adapter for tenantSchema / schema-qualified handling returns nothing, so the detector has no concept of a schema qualifier as a tenant predicate. The sibling UPDATE in the same function (lines 765-773, `UPDATE \\\"${tenantSchema}\\\".edge_devices ... WHERE id = $1`) follows the same schema-qualified convention. The adapter's own fixture already records judge-diagnosed false-positive classes for this rule (semantic-raw-query-predicates.json:19, `predicated_raw_query`); this finding is a sibling class, schema-qualified raw query. Root-cause change in the detector (Tier 3, make the correct behaviour detectable): extend callHasTenantPredicate at tenant-scoping-adapter.ts:231 to recognise a tenant-schema-qualified table reference (an interpolated identifier derived from getTenantSchemaName / tenantSchema in the statement text) and add a `schema_qualified_raw_query` false_positive_class with a fixture case, so the rule stops
